**Pikachu** ([Japanese](/wiki/List_of_Japanese_Pok%C3%A9mon_names "List of Japanese Pokémon names"): **ピカチュウ** *Pikachu*) is an [Electric-type](/wiki/Electric_(type) "Electric (type)") [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_(species) "Pokémon (species)") introduced in [Generation I](/wiki/Generation_I "Generation I").

It [evolves](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") from [Pichu](/wiki/Pichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pichu (Pokémon)") when [leveled up with high friendship](/wiki/Friendship_Evolution "Friendship Evolution") and evolves into [Raichu](/wiki/Raichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raichu (Pokémon)") when exposed to a [Thunder Stone](/wiki/Thunder_Stone "Thunder Stone").

In [Alola](/wiki/Alola "Alola"), Pikachu evolves into [Alolan](/wiki/Alolan_form "Alolan form") Raichu when exposed to a Thunder Stone.

Pikachu has sixteen alternate [forms](#Form_data) that fall into four groups: **[Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu")**, **[Pikachu in a cap](/wiki/Pikachu_in_a_cap "Pikachu in a cap")**, the **[partner](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!) "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)") Pikachu**, and **Gigantamax Pikachu**. Ordinary Pikachu can [Gigantamax](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax") into Gigantamax Pikachu if it has the [Gigantamax Factor](/wiki/Gigantamax#Gigantamax_Factor "Gigantamax"). Additionally, many other [Pikachu variants](/wiki/Pikachu_variants "Pikachu variants") have appeared in various media.

Cosplay Pikachu, Pikachu in a cap, the partner Pikachu, and Pikachu with the Gigantamax Factor cannot evolve. The [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Yellow) "Pikachu (Yellow)") received at the beginning of [Pokémon Yellow Version](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version") will refuse to evolve into Raichu unless it is [traded](/wiki/Trade "Trade") and evolved on another save file.

Pikachu is popularly known as the mascot of the [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon "Pokémon") franchise and one of [Nintendo](/wiki/Nintendo "Nintendo")'s major mascots. It is also the [game mascot](/wiki/Game_mascot "Game mascot") and the [player's first Pokémon](/wiki/List_of_the_player%27s_first_Pok%C3%A9mon "List of the player's first Pokémon") in [Pokémon: Yellow Version: Special Pikachu Edition](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version") and [Let's Go, Pikachu!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon:_Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee! "Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!"), as well as in [Pokémon Rumble Blast](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Rumble_Blast "Pokémon Rumble Blast") and [Pokémon Rumble World](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Rumble_World "Pokémon Rumble World"), and has made numerous appearances on the covers of spin-off games.

## Biology

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/0/05/HOME0845Go.png/200px-HOME0845Go.png)](/wiki/File:HOME0845Go.png)A Pikachu who has been accidentally partially [swallowed](/wiki/Gulp_Missile_(Ability) "Gulp Missile (Ability)") by a [Cramorant](/wiki/Cramorant_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Cramorant (Pokémon)")

Pikachu is a short, chubby [rodent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/rodent "wp:rodent") [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_(species) "Pokémon (species)"). It is covered in yellow fur with two horizontal brown stripes on its back. It has a small mouth, long, pointed ears with black tips, and brown eyes. Each cheek is a red circle that contains a pouch for electricity storage. It has short forearms with five fingers on each paw, and its feet each have three toes. At the base of its lightning bolt-shaped tail is a patch of brown fur. A [female](/wiki/List_of_Pok%C3%A9mon_with_gender_differences "List of Pokémon with gender differences") Pikachu has a "V"-shaped notch at the end of its tail, which looks like the top of a heart. It is classified as a quadruped, but it has been known to stand and walk on its hind legs; therefore, Pikachu is a facultative biped.

Pikachu stores electric energy using the small pouches in its cheeks in case it is attacked, so one might feel a small shock if it is touched. These pouches become electrically charged during the night as it sleeps, so a dozy Pikachu may occasionally discharge electricity after waking up. If it does not get enough sleep, it will not be able to release said electricity at its full power. When angered or threatened, Pikachu quickly unleashes the electric energy from its cheeks, which can reach the intensity of a lightning bolt. This also serves as a sign that it's wary, and it will feel stressed if it's unable to fully release the electricity. In *[Sparks Fly for Magnemite](/wiki/EP030 "EP030")*, Pikachu is shown to accumulate energy in its glands, which it will need to eject to avoid complications. When several Pikachu form a group, they are capable of causing thunder storms by building up electricity. If one Pikachu sees another one who is weakened and in need of help, it revitalizes it by recharging it with an electric shock. However, as seen with [Peakychu](/wiki/Peakychu "Peakychu"), overusing this ability alters its physical structure, causing it to lose its ability to generate electricity and its fur to become pale. In [Alola](/wiki/Alola "Alola"), a plan was recently announced to help build an electric power plant by gathering many Pikachu. If its cheek sacs are very soft and stretchy, this means that it can generate powerful electricity. As shown in [Pokémon Sleep](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Sleep "Pokémon Sleep"), Pikachu is known to emit electricity through its cheeks while sleeping, perhaps because it's dreaming of firing electric shots.[[1]](#cite_note-1)

Pikachu is a curious, playful, and intelligent Pokémon who primarily eats fruits and roasts [Berries](/wiki/Berry "Berry") with its electricity to make them tender enough to eat. However, it can end up accidentally burning them too much if it underestimates the intensity of its charge, as it has a tendency to blast new things it finds. In [The Electric Tale of Pikachu](/wiki/The_Electric_Tale_of_Pikachu "The Electric Tale of Pikachu"), it is also depicted eating and sometimes destroying telephone poles, wires, and other electronic equipment. Pikachu raises its tail to monitor its surroundings and bites [people](/wiki/Human "Human") who yank it, as it does not like having it pulled. While doing this, its tail is sometimes struck by lightning. [Wild](/wiki/Wild_Pok%C3%A9mon "Wild Pokémon") Pikachu live in [forested areas](/wiki/List_of_Pok%C3%A9mon_by_habitat#Forest_Pok%C3%A9mon "List of Pokémon by habitat") away from people and with others of its kind in nests, as seen in the *[Pokémon the Series](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series "Pokémon the Series")* episode *[Pikachu's Goodbye](/wiki/EP039 "EP039")*. Such forests are dangerous, though, as trees there are often struck by lightning. When Pikachu meet, their tails make contact and they exchange electricity through them to greet each other. Relatedly, in *[A Plethora of Pikachu!](/wiki/SM091 "SM091")*, it is shown that Pikachu from Alola are known to greet each other by sniffing one another and rubbing their tails together. In the [Pokémon Adventures](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Adventures "Pokémon Adventures") manga, it is considered exceptionally rare, though it seems to be a quite common Pokémon in other forms of media. Many [Pokémon Trainers](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer "Pokémon Trainer") prefer Pikachu's cute appearance over [Raichu](/wiki/Raichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raichu (Pokémon)")'s, which is why the latter is not commonly seen among them. Because of the popularity of Pikachu-themed merchandise, [Mimikyu](/wiki/Mimikyu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Mimikyu (Pokémon)") dresses itself in a Pikachu cloth in an effort to look less scary and make friends and appease its loneliness.[[2]](#cite_note-2) Unfortunately, its creepy costume only ends up doing the opposite. By occasion, Pikachu can get unintentionally [swallowed](/wiki/Gulp_Missile_(Ability) "Gulp Missile (Ability)") by [Cramorant](/wiki/Cramorant_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Cramorant (Pokémon)"). While startled, it looks for ways to escape from the latter. Pikachu, along with [Pichu](/wiki/Pichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pichu (Pokémon)") and Raichu, is related to [Pachirisu](/wiki/Pachirisu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pachirisu (Pokémon)").

Pikachu and its [evolutionary](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") relatives are the [only known Pokémon](/wiki/Signature_move "Signature move") capable of learning the [move](/wiki/Move "Move") [Volt Tackle](/wiki/Volt_Tackle_(move) "Volt Tackle (move)"). It is also the only known Pokémon capable of using the [Z-Move](/wiki/Z-Move "Z-Move") [Catastropika](/wiki/Catastropika_(move) "Catastropika (move)"), as well as the only one who can use the [Light Ball](/wiki/Light_Ball "Light Ball") [item](/wiki/Item "Item").  


### Forms

Pikachu has sixteen additional forms that fall into four groups: [Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu"), [Pikachu in a cap](/wiki/Pikachu_in_a_cap "Pikachu in a cap"), the [partner](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!) "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)") Pikachu, and [Gigantamax](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax") Pikachu. Additionally, many other [Pikachu variants](/wiki/Pikachu_variants "Pikachu variants") have appeared in various media.

#### Cosplay Pikachu

[Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu") is always [female](/wiki/Gender "Gender"), has a black heart-shaped spot at the end of its tail, and can be dressed in any of five outfits corresponding to the five [Contest conditions](/wiki/Contest_conditions "Contest conditions"): [Cool](/wiki/Cool "Cool"), [Beautiful](/wiki/Beautiful "Beautiful"), [Cute](/wiki/Cute "Cute"), [Clever](/wiki/Clever "Clever"), and [Tough](/wiki/Tough "Tough").

- [Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu") [Pikachu Rock Star](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu") [Pikachu Belle](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu") [Pikachu Pop Star](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu") [Pikachu, Ph.D](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu") [Pikachu Libre](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu")
- [/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Cosplay.png](/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Cosplay.png) [/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Rock_Star.png](/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Rock_Star.png) [/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Belle.png](/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Belle.png) [/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Pop_Star.png](/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Pop_Star.png) [/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-PhD.png](/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-PhD.png) [/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Libre.png](/wiki/File:0025Pikachu-Libre.png)

#### Pikachu in a cap

[Pikachu in a cap](/wiki/Pikachu_in_a_cap "Pikachu in a cap") is always male and has eight variants, each wearing one of [Ash Ketchum](/wiki/Ash_Ketchum "Ash Ketchum")'s [hats](/wiki/Ash%27s_hat "Ash's hat") from [*Pokémon the Series*](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series "Pokémon the Series"). This form of Pikachu is somewhat rare and wears the hat of its partner, who is its [Trainer](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer "Pokémon Trainer"). The cap is a bit loose because it is not the right size, but brims with memories of Pikachu and its Trainer's first encounter and is proof that they have traveled through many [regions](/wiki/Region "Region") together.

Pikachu in a cap is the only known Pokémon capable of using the Z-Move [10,000,000 Volt Thunderbolt](/wiki/10,000,000_Volt_Thunderbolt_(move) "10,000,000 Volt Thunderbolt (move)"), but cannot use [Catastropika](/wiki/Catastropika_(move) "Catastropika (move)") or [Gigantamax](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax").

#### Partner Pikachu

The [partner](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!) "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)") Pikachu is the Pokémon the player starts with in [Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon:_Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee! "Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!"). It can be either gender and has higher [base stats](/wiki/Base_stat "Base stat") than normal Pikachu. The partner Pikachu is not differentiated from standard Pikachu in [Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon:_Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee! "Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!")'s [Pokédex](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9dex "Pokédex"), and it cannot be [transferred](/wiki/Transfer "Transfer") to other games.

The partner Pikachu is the only known Pokémon capable of learning the moves [Zippy Zap](/wiki/Zippy_Zap_(move) "Zippy Zap (move)"), [Floaty Fall](/wiki/Floaty_Fall_(move) "Floaty Fall (move)"), and [Splishy Splash](/wiki/Splishy_Splash_(move) "Splishy Splash (move)"), as well as the [Partner Power](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!)#Exclusive_moves "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)") [Pika Papow](/wiki/Pika_Papow_(move) "Pika Papow (move)").

#### Gigantamax Pikachu

As [Gigantamax](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax") Pikachu, it becomes larger and chubbier, and its tail becomes longer with three red clouds floating around it.

The Gigantamax phenomenon expands Pikachu's power and forms its massive body and tail. As a result, the might of Gigantamax Pikachu's electricity is equal to that of a power plant; despite this, it is difficult to use to power peoples' homes since it can only remain in this form for a short amount of time. All the electricity it produces in its cheek sacs goes to its tail, enabling it to attack foes in [battles](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_battle "Pokémon battle") with electric jolts as powerful as lightning strikes.[[3]](#cite_note-3)

Gigantamax Pikachu is the only known Pokémon capable of using the [G-Max Move](/wiki/G-Max_Move "G-Max Move") [G-Max Volt Crash](/wiki/G-Max_Volt_Crash_(move) "G-Max Volt Crash (move)").

They resemble tanned Pikachu with clothes similar to the playable character.

### Evolution

Pikachu [evolves](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") from [Pichu](/wiki/Pichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pichu (Pokémon)") and evolves into [Raichu](/wiki/Raichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raichu (Pokémon)").

In [Alola](/wiki/Alola "Alola"), it evolves into [Alolan](/wiki/Alolan_form "Alolan form") Raichu.

[Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu"), [Pikachu in a cap](/wiki/Pikachu_in_a_cap "Pikachu in a cap"), [Partner Pikachu](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!) "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)"), and Pikachu with the [Gigantamax Factor](/wiki/Gigantamax#Gigantamax_Factor "Gigantamax") cannot evolve.

(For specifics on this Pokémon's Evolution in the games, refer to [Game data→Evolution data](#Evolution_data).)

## Game data

### As the player

*Main article: [Pikachu (PokéPark)](/wiki/Pikachu_(Pok%C3%A9Park) "Pikachu (PokéPark)")*

Pikachu appears as the [player character](/wiki/Player_character "Player character") in both [PokéPark Wii: Pikachu's Adventure](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9Park_Wii:_Pikachu%27s_Adventure "PokéPark Wii: Pikachu's Adventure") and its sequel, [PokéPark 2: Wonders Beyond](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9Park_2:_Wonders_Beyond "PokéPark 2: Wonders Beyond"). He falls down a mysterious tunnel one day with his friends [Charmander](/wiki/Charmander_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Charmander (Pokémon)"), [Chikorita](/wiki/Chikorita_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Chikorita (Pokémon)"), and [Piplup](/wiki/Piplup_(Pok%C3%A9Park) "Piplup (PokéPark)") and ends up in the [PokéPark](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9Park_(game) "PokéPark (game)"). There, he goes on a quest to save the PokéPark from certain destruction. He later travels to a new PokéPark, which is being threatened by the ever-expanding [Wish Park](/wiki/Wish_Park "Wish Park"). At the new PokéPark, he meets [Snivy](/wiki/Snivy_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Snivy (Pokémon)"), [Tepig](/wiki/Tepig_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Tepig (Pokémon)"), and [Oshawott](/wiki/Oshawott_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Oshawott (Pokémon)"), and they help to save it from danger.

### NPC appearances

#### In the core series

- These notable [Pokémon Trainers](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer "Pokémon Trainer") all use Pikachu:
  * [Lt. Surge](/wiki/Lt._Surge "Lt. Surge") uses a Pikachu in [Pokémon Red, Green](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Red_and_Green_Versions "Pokémon Red and Green Versions"), [Blue](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Blue_Version "Pokémon Blue Version"), [FireRed, and LeafGreen](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_FireRed_and_LeafGreen_Versions "Pokémon FireRed and LeafGreen Versions").
  * [Red](/wiki/Red_(game) "Red (game)"), who makes NPC appearances in [Generation II](/wiki/Generation_II "Generation II"), [HeartGold, SoulSilver](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_HeartGold_and_SoulSilver_Versions "Pokémon HeartGold and SoulSilver Versions"), [Black 2, White 2](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Black_and_White_Versions_2 "Pokémon Black and White Versions 2"), and [Generation VII](/wiki/Generation_VII "Generation VII"), owns a [Pikachu](/wiki/Red%27s_Pikachu "Red's Pikachu"), typically as his highest [leveled](/wiki/Level "Level") Pokémon. Usually, he represents the [player](/wiki/Player_character "Player character") from the [Kanto](/wiki/Kanto "Kanto")-based games, particularly [Pokémon Yellow](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version") in which his [first partner Pokémon](/wiki/First_partner_Pok%C3%A9mon "First partner Pokémon") was a Pikachu. Starting in HeartGold and SoulSilver, Pikachu will always be holding a [Light Ball](/wiki/Light_Ball "Light Ball") when applicable.
  * [Wattson](/wiki/Wattson "Wattson") uses a Pikachu exclusively in his second rematch in [Pokémon Emerald](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Emerald_Version "Pokémon Emerald Version"). In subsequent [battles](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_battle "Pokémon battle"), it has [evolved](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") into a [Raichu](/wiki/Raichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raichu (Pokémon)").
  * [Trevor](/wiki/Trevor "Trevor") uses his Pikachu during the main story of [Pokémon X and Y](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_X_and_Y "Pokémon X and Y"). In his second battle against the player, it has evolved into a Raichu.
  * [Hau](/wiki/Hau "Hau")'s main partner in [Pokémon Sun, Moon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Sun_and_Moon "Pokémon Sun and Moon"), [Ultra Sun, and Ultra Moon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Ultra_Sun_and_Ultra_Moon "Pokémon Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon") is a Pikachu who eventually evolves into an [Alolan](/wiki/Alolan_form "Alolan form") Raichu. It is first seen as a [Pichu](/wiki/Pichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pichu (Pokémon)").
  * [Trace](/wiki/Trace_(game) "Trace (game)")'s main partner in [Pokémon Let's Go, Eevee!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon:_Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee! "Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!") is a Pikachu which later evolves into a Raichu.
  * The player's [friend](/wiki/Friend "Friend") in [Pokémon Legends: Arceus](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Legends:_Arceus "Pokémon Legends: Arceus"), either [Rei](/wiki/Rei_(game) "Rei (game)") or [Akari](/wiki/Akari_(game) "Akari (game)") (depending on the player's [gender](/wiki/Gender "Gender")), has a Pikachu. It is seen as their main partner and will often appear outside its [Poké Ball](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9_Ball_(Hisui) "Poké Ball (Hisui)").
  * The [Poké Kid](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9_Kid_(Trainer_class) "Poké Kid (Trainer class)") [Trainer class](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer#Trainer_class "Pokémon Trainer") is known to use Pikachu. In [Pokémon Sword and Shield](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Sword_and_Shield "Pokémon Sword and Shield"), only male Poké Kids will use Pikachu as a parallel to the female Poké Kids, who use [Eevee](/wiki/Eevee_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Eevee (Pokémon)").
- [Ikue Ohtani](/wiki/Ikue_Ohtani "Ikue Ohtani"), the voice of Pikachu, makes cameo appearances in Pokémon Gold, Silver, HeartGold and SoulSilver (all as [Picknicker](/wiki/Picknicker "Picknicker") Brooke), in X and Y (as [Breeder](/wiki/Breeder "Breeder") Mercy), and in all [Generation VII](/wiki/Generation_VII "Generation VII") games, where she owns a Pikachu. In [Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon:_Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee! "Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!"), Ikue is the [Master Trainer](/wiki/Master_Trainer "Master Trainer") for Pikachu.
- Pikachu has appeared in the overworld in many titles as interactable NPCs.
- Several images of Pikachu show up in [Totem](/wiki/Totem_Pok%C3%A9mon "Totem Pokémon") [Mimikyu](/wiki/Mimikyu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Mimikyu (Pokémon)")'s chamber, notably [Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu") and [Pikachu Pop Star](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu").


#### In the spin-off games

- [Pokémon Stadium](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Stadium "Pokémon Stadium"): Pikachu stars in the mini-game "Thundering Dynamo" alongside [Voltorb](/wiki/Voltorb_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Voltorb (Pokémon)"). This mini-game involves charging up electric power.
- [Hey You, Pikachu!](/wiki/Hey_You,_Pikachu! "Hey You, Pikachu!"): Pikachu stars alongside an unnamed child (who bears a striking resemblance to Red) who was recently taught how to interact with wild [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_(species) "Pokémon (species)") by [Professor Oak](/wiki/Professor_Oak "Professor Oak"). The boy and Pikachu form a bond and go on many adventures together, and one day, Pikachu decides to live with the boy.
- [Pokémon Stadium 2](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Stadium_2 "Pokémon Stadium 2"): Pikachu can be used in "Pichu's Power Plant" if it is [transferred](/wiki/Transfer "Transfer") from another game.
- [Pokémon Pinball](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Pinball "Pokémon Pinball"): Pikachu serves as a Ball-Saver, but it will only work if the Lightning Meter is full.
- [Pokémon Pinball: Ruby & Sapphire](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Pinball:_Ruby_%26_Sapphire "Pokémon Pinball: Ruby &amp; Sapphire"): Pikachu has the same role as in Pokémon Pinball, but is sometimes helped by Pichu. Pikachu also appears on the Catch 'Em Mode banner.
- [Pokémon Channel](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Channel "Pokémon Channel"): Much like Hey You, Pikachu!, this game focuses on a boy and a Pikachu bonding through social interactions, such as going outside and talking to other Pokémon, and watching TV together.
- [Pokémon Pokopia](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Pokopia "Pokémon Pokopia"): A Pikachu named [Peakychu](/wiki/Peakychu "Peakychu") is a supporting character in the game. Due to using up large amounts of her own electricity to aid her friends, she has become abnormally pale and can no longer generate electricity without outside assistance.

### [Held items](/wiki/Held_item "Held item")

Pokémon caught in [Generation I](/wiki/Generation_I "Generation I") must be [traded](/wiki/Trade "Trade") to a [Generation II](/wiki/Generation_II "Generation II") game in order for a held item to appear. In [Pokémon Yellow](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version"), the player gets the [Light Ball](/wiki/Light_Ball "Light Ball") by trading the [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Yellow) "Pikachu (Yellow)") received at the beginning of the game, otherwise the Pikachu will be holding a Berry. In [Pokéwalker](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9walker "Pokéwalker"), only Pikachu found in [Yellow Forest](/wiki/Yellow_Forest "Yellow Forest") are carrying items.

- **[Photodex](/wiki/List_of_Pok%C3%A9mon_by_Photodex_order "List of Pokémon by Photodex order") entry #030**
- *Pikachu build up electricity in their cheek sacs while they sleep. They’re also very curious—you’ll often see one playing at our [research camp](/wiki/Laboratory_of_Ecology_and_Natural_Sciences "Laboratory of Ecology and Natural Sciences").* | | | | | |
| - **[Pokémon Sleep](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Sleep "Pokémon Sleep")**

- [/wiki/File:Sleep_Menu_0025.png](/wiki/File:Sleep_Menu_0025.png) **[Sleep type](/wiki/Sleep_type "Sleep type"):** Snoozing **Pokémon type:** [Electric](/wiki/Electric_(type) "Electric (type)") **[Points to befriend](/wiki/Biscuit "Biscuit"):** 7
- **Specialty:** Berries **Berry:** [/wiki/File:Sleep_Grepa_Berry.png](/wiki/File:Sleep_Grepa_Berry.png)[Grepa Berry](/wiki/Grepa_Berry "Grepa Berry") ×2 **[Primary ingredient](/wiki/Cooking_(Sleep) "Cooking (Sleep)"):** [/wiki/File:Sleep_Fancy_Apple.png](/wiki/File:Sleep_Fancy_Apple.png)Fancy Apple ×1

- [/wiki/File:Sleep_Fancy_Apple.png](/wiki/File:Sleep_Fancy_Apple.png)Fancy Apple [/wiki/File:Sleep_Fancy_Egg.png](/wiki/File:Sleep_Fancy_Egg.png)Fancy Egg [/wiki/File:Sleep_Warming_Ginger.png](/wiki/File:Sleep_Warming_Ginger.png)Warming Ginger

- While sleeping, it generates electricity in its cheek sacs. Apparently, it may also discharge electricity while it dozes, as if firing off electric shocks in its dreams.

### Form data

Pikachu has sixteen additional forms that fall into four groups: [Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu"), [Pikachu in a cap](/wiki/Pikachu_in_a_cap "Pikachu in a cap"), the [partner](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!) "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)") Pikachu, and [Gigantamax](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax") Pikachu. All forms except the partner Pikachu are recognized by an in-game [Pokédex](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9dex "Pokédex"); partner Pikachu has only been named in promotional materials.[[4]](#cite_note-4)

#### Cosplay Pikachu

[Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu") is the base form of a group of six interchangeable forms exclusive to [Pokémon Omega Ruby and Alpha Sapphire](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Omega_Ruby_and_Alpha_Sapphire "Pokémon Omega Ruby and Alpha Sapphire"). It is a [female](/wiki/Gender "Gender") Pikachu who has a black, heart-shaped mark at the end of its tail, obtained from a [Pokémon Breeder](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Breeder_(Trainer_class) "Pokémon Breeder (Trainer class)") after the player takes part in their first [Pokémon Contest Spectacular](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Contest "Pokémon Contest"). Cosplay Pikachu can change forms by dressing up in different costumes corresponding to each of the five [contest conditions](/wiki/Contest_condition "Contest condition") ([Coolness](/wiki/Cool_(condition) "Cool (condition)"), [Beauty](/wiki/Beautiful_(condition) "Beautiful (condition)"), [Cuteness](/wiki/Cute_(condition) "Cute (condition)"), [Cleverness](/wiki/Clever_(condition) "Clever (condition)"), and [Toughness](/wiki/Tough_(condition) "Tough (condition)")); each form also automatically learns a [move](/wiki/Move "Move") that Pikachu otherwise could not learn. Cosplay Pikachu can change or remove its costume at any [Contest Hall](/wiki/Contest_Hall "Contest Hall"), forgetting the move it learned with the last costume (if it still knows it) and learning the move from the new costume (if applicable). If Cosplay Pikachu removes its costume while the only move it knows is the move tied to its costume, it learns [Thunder Shock](/wiki/Thunder_Shock_(move) "Thunder Shock (move)").

Unlike other Pikachu, Cosplay Pikachu cannot [evolve](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") or [breed](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_breeding "Pokémon breeding"). It also cannot be [traded](/wiki/Trade "Trade") via the [GTS](/wiki/Global_Trade_System "Global Trade System") or [Wonder Trade](/wiki/Wonder_Trade "Wonder Trade"). Additionally, as it is not present in any games besides Omega Ruby and Alpha Sapphire, Cosplay Pikachu cannot be deposited into [Pokémon Bank](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Bank "Pokémon Bank"), and it does not register as a separate form in Pokémon Bank's [Pokédex](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9dex "Pokédex"), despite it registering separately in the Pokédex of Omega Ruby and Alpha Sapphire.

#### Pikachu in a cap

[Pikachu in a cap](/wiki/Pikachu_in_a_cap "Pikachu in a cap") are eight [event](/wiki/Event_Pok%C3%A9mon "Event Pokémon")-exclusive, non-interchangeable forms of Pikachu that debuted in [Pokémon Sun and Moon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Sun_and_Moon "Pokémon Sun and Moon"). Like [Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu"), these Pikachu are always [male](/wiki/Gender "Gender"), and they cannot [evolve](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") or [breed](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_breeding "Pokémon breeding"). They are also able to use the event-exclusive [Z-Crystal](/wiki/Z-Crystal "Z-Crystal") [Pikashunium Z](/wiki/Pikashunium_Z "Pikashunium Z") and its corresponding [Z-Move](/wiki/Z-Move "Z-Move") [10,000,000 Volt Thunderbolt](/wiki/10,000,000_Volt_Thunderbolt_(move) "10,000,000 Volt Thunderbolt (move)"). Their [cries](/wiki/Cry "Cry") differ from that of regular Pikachu. They are not present in [Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon:_Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee! "Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!"), [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond and Shining Pearl](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Brilliant_Diamond_and_Shining_Pearl "Pokémon Brilliant Diamond and Shining Pearl"), [Pokémon Legends: Arceus](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Legends:_Arceus "Pokémon Legends: Arceus"), or [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Legends:_Z-A "Pokémon Legends: Z-A").

Six variants exist in Pokémon Sun and Moon, one for each of [Ash's hats](/wiki/Ash%27s_hat "Ash's hat") from the [original series](/wiki/Original_series "Original series") to [*Pokémon the Series: Sun & Moon*](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series:_Sun_%26_Moon "Pokémon the Series: Sun &amp; Moon"). A seventh variant debuted in [Pokémon Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Ultra_Sun_and_Ultra_Moon "Pokémon Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon"), wearing the Partner Cap, Ash's hat from *[Pokémon the Movie: I Choose You!](/wiki/M20 "M20")*. An eighth variant debuted in the Version 1.2.0 update of [Pokémon Sword and Shield](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Sword_and_Shield "Pokémon Sword and Shield"), wearing the World Cap, Ash's hat from [*Pokémon Journeys: The Series*](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Journeys:_The_Series "Pokémon Journeys: The Series").

The original six Pikachu in a cap were distributed via [serial codes](/wiki/Serial_code "Serial code") in Pokémon Sun and Moon in celebration of the 20th [Pokémon movie](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_movie "Pokémon movie"), and by extension, the 20th anniversary of *[Pokémon the Series](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series "Pokémon the Series")*. Partner Cap Pikachu was distributed in Pokémon Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon via a [QR Code](/wiki/QR_Code "QR Code") that can be [scanned](/wiki/QR_Scanner "QR Scanner"). All eight Pikachu in a cap (including World Cap Pikachu) were distributed via serial codes in Pokémon Sword and Shield to commemorate the TV show's [timeslot change](/wiki/JN040 "JN040") to Fridays in Japan; uniquely, the Pikachu from this distribution can learn [Volt Tackle](/wiki/Volt_Tackle_(move) "Volt Tackle (move)") from the [Move Reminder](/wiki/Move_Reminder "Move Reminder").

- [Original Cap](/wiki/Original_series "Original series") [Hoenn Cap](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series:_Ruby_and_Sapphire "Pokémon the Series: Ruby and Sapphire") [Sinnoh Cap](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series:_Diamond_and_Pearl "Pokémon the Series: Diamond and Pearl") [Unova Cap](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series:_Black_%26_White "Pokémon the Series: Black &amp; White") [Kalos Cap](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series:_XY "Pokémon the Series: XY") [Alola Cap](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series:_Sun_%26_Moon "Pokémon the Series: Sun &amp; Moon") [Partner Cap](/wiki/M20 "M20") [World Cap](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Journeys:_The_Series "Pokémon Journeys: The Series")

#### Partner Pikachu

The [partner](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!) "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)") Pikachu has a separate [internal index](/wiki/List_of_Pok%C3%A9mon_by_index_number_in_Generation_VII "List of Pokémon by index number in Generation VII") from standard Pikachu. This allows it to have higher [base stats](/wiki/Base_stats "Base stats") than wild Pikachu.

The partner Pikachu is not differentiated from standard Pikachu in [Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon:_Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee! "Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!")'s [Pokédex](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9dex "Pokédex"), and it cannot be [transferred](/wiki/Transfer "Transfer") to other games.

### Evolution data

[Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu"), [Pikachu in a cap](/wiki/Pikachu_in_a_cap "Pikachu in a cap"), the [partner](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!) "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)") Pikachu, and Pikachu with the [Gigantamax Factor](/wiki/Gigantamax#Gigantamax_Factor "Gigantamax") cannot [evolve](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution"). The [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Yellow) "Pikachu (Yellow)") received at the beginning of [Pokémon Yellow](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version") will refuse to evolve into [Raichu](/wiki/Raichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raichu (Pokémon)") unless it is [traded](/wiki/Trade "Trade") and evolved on another save file.

## In animation

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/f/f1/Pikachu_gender_difference_anime.png/250px-Pikachu_gender_difference_anime.png)](/wiki/File:Pikachu_gender_difference_anime.png)[Male and female](/wiki/Gender "Gender") Pikachu in [*Pokémon Journeys: The Series*](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Journeys:_The_Series "Pokémon Journeys: The Series")[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/b/b2/Roy_Captain_Pikachu.png/250px-Roy_Captain_Pikachu.png)](/wiki/File:Roy_Captain_Pikachu.png)Captain Pikachu in [*Pokémon Horizons: The Series*](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Horizons:_The_Series "Pokémon Horizons: The Series")[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/1/12/Captain_Pikachu_Terastal.png/250px-Captain_Pikachu_Terastal.png)](/wiki/File:Captain_Pikachu_Terastal.png)[Tera](/wiki/Terastal_phenomenon "Terastal phenomenon") Pikachu in *Pokémon Horizons: The Series*

### Main series

#### Major appearances

##### [Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu")

[Ash Ketchum](/wiki/Ash_Ketchum "Ash Ketchum") has a Pikachu that he obtained from [Professor Oak](/wiki/Professor_Oak_(anime) "Professor Oak (anime)") in *[Pokémon - I Choose You!](/wiki/EP001 "EP001")*, the first episode of [*Pokémon the Series*](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series "Pokémon the Series"), which also marked the species' debut. He was the main [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_(species) "Pokémon (species)") character of the animated series from the [original series](/wiki/Original_series "Original series") to [*Pokémon Journeys: The Series*](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Journeys:_The_Series "Pokémon Journeys: The Series") and appeared in almost every non-special episode and every [Pokémon movie](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_movie "Pokémon movie") released throughout that point. In *[The Climb to Be the Very Best!](/wiki/JN013 "JN013")*, he was revealed to be capable of [Gigantamaxing](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax"). He always [remains outside](/wiki/Walking_Pok%C3%A9mon "Walking Pokémon") of his [Poké Ball](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9_Ball_(item) "Poké Ball (item)").

##### [Pikachutwo](/wiki/Pikachutwo "Pikachutwo")

In *[Mewtwo Strikes Back](/wiki/M01 "M01")*, [Mewtwo](/wiki/Mewtwo_(M01) "Mewtwo (M01)") cloned Ash's Pikachu. This Pikachu can be distinguished from Ash's due to the spikes in the black marks found on the tips of its ears. It is also comparatively more aggressive than Ash's Pikachu and slurs its speech, defects possibly caused by Ash tampering with Mewtwo's cloning machine during the cloning process. It reappeared in the sequel special *[Mewtwo Returns](/wiki/Mewtwo_Returns "Mewtwo Returns")* and the remake movie *[Mewtwo Strikes Back—Evolution](/wiki/M22 "M22")*.

##### [Puka](/wiki/Puka "Puka")

A Pikachu [nicknamed](/wiki/Nickname "Nickname") Puka was owned by [Victor](/wiki/Victor_(EP067) "Victor (EP067)") on [Seafoam Island](/wiki/Seafoam_Island_(anime) "Seafoam Island (anime)") in *[The Pi-Kahuna](/wiki/EP067 "EP067")*. This blue-eyed Pikachu saved Ash from drowning by having the ability to sense tidal waves approaching.

##### [Sparky](/wiki/Sparky "Sparky")

[Ritchie](/wiki/Ritchie "Ritchie") has a Pikachu nicknamed Sparky, who debuted in *[A Friend In Deed](/wiki/EP078 "EP078")*. Sparky, unlike most Pikachu, has a tuft of fur on the top of its head, and, unlike Ash's Pikachu, has no problem with living inside a Poké Ball.

##### [Ashachu](/wiki/Ashachu "Ashachu")

Ash was [turned into a Pikachu](/wiki/Human%E2%80%93Pok%C3%A9mon_transformation "Human–Pokémon transformation") in *[Hocus Pokémon](/wiki/EP241 "EP241")* by a Pokémon magician named [Lily](/wiki/Lily_(EP241) "Lily (EP241)"). He reverted to his human self at the beginning of [the next episode](/wiki/EP242 "EP242").

##### [Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu")

All of the Cosplay Pikachu appeared in *[Lights! Camera! Pika!](/wiki/XY078 "XY078")*, under the ownership of [Frank](/wiki/Frank "Frank"). They reappeared in *[Hoopa and the Clash of Ages](/wiki/M18 "M18")*, where they were all summoned by [Hoopa](/wiki/Hoopa_(anime) "Hoopa (anime)") as part of a prank on Ash and his Pikachu.

##### [Ash's Pikachu (M20)](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu_(M20) "Ash's Pikachu (M20)")

In the *[I Choose You!](/wiki/M20 "M20")* [alternate continuity](/wiki/I_Choose_You!_trilogy "I Choose You! trilogy"), [Ash Ketchum](/wiki/Ash_Ketchum_(M20) "Ash Ketchum (M20)") has a Pikachu who is his first Pokémon and his main partner, almost always remaining outside of his Poké Ball.

##### [Goh's Pikachu](/wiki/Goh%27s_Pikachu "Goh's Pikachu")

[Goh](/wiki/Goh "Goh") [caught](/wiki/Caught_Pok%C3%A9mon "Caught Pokémon") a Pikachu in [*Gotta Catch a* What?!](/wiki/JN035 "JN035"). She, along with a few others of her kind, later evolved into Raichu during the episode.

##### [Captain Pikachu](/wiki/Captain_Pikachu "Captain Pikachu")

In *[The Pendant That Starts It All (Part Two)](/wiki/HZ002 "HZ002")*, a Pikachu nicknamed Captain Pikachu appeared under the ownership of [Friede](/wiki/Friede "Friede"). It is capable of [Terastallizing](/wiki/Terastal_phenomenon "Terastal phenomenon") into a [Flying](/wiki/Flying_(type) "Flying (type)") [type](/wiki/Type "Type"), as first seen in [HZ121](/wiki/HZ121 "HZ121").

##### Other

In *[Pikachu's Goodbye](/wiki/EP039 "EP039")*, many wild Pikachu appear and Ash's Pikachu wants to play with them. Eventually, Ash tries to release him thinking it'd be best for Pikachu to be with his own kind, but he refused to leave Ash's side.

In *[The Ultimate Test](/wiki/EP056 "EP056")*, [James](/wiki/James "James") used a Pikachu in the [Pokémon League Admissions Exam](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_League_Admissions_Exam "Pokémon League Admissions Exam"), but it was defeated by the [instructor](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_League_entrance_exam_instructor "Pokémon League entrance exam instructor")'s [Graveler](/wiki/Graveler_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Graveler (Pokémon)"). After interfering with a [battle](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_battle "Pokémon battle") Ash was having with the instructor, James later tried to steal this Pikachu, only for it to zap James instead, as instructed by its instructor.

In *[Pokémon Mystery Dungeon: Team Go-Getters Out of the Gate!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Mystery_Dungeon:_Team_Go-Getters_Out_of_the_Gate! "Pokémon Mystery Dungeon: Team Go-Getters Out of the Gate!")*, a Pikachu was kidnapped by a [Skarmory](/wiki/Skarmory_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Skarmory (Pokémon)"). As a result, [Team Go-Getters](/wiki/Team_Go-Getters "Team Go-Getters") set out to rescue him as their first mission. Pikachu provided them with some items in the battle.

In [BWS01](/wiki/BWS01 "BWS01"), [Ayumi](/wiki/Ayumi "Ayumi")'s [female](/wiki/Gender "Gender") Pikachu was trained on how to properly use [Thunderbolt](/wiki/Thunderbolt_(move) "Thunderbolt (move)") with the help of [Cilan](/wiki/Cilan_(anime) "Cilan (anime)") and [Stunfisk](/wiki/Cilan%27s_Stunfisk "Cilan's Stunfisk").

A [Mirror World](/wiki/Mirror_World "Mirror World") Pikachu appeared in *[The Cave of Mirrors!](/wiki/XY037 "XY037")*, under the ownership of [Mirror Ash](/wiki/Mirror_Ash "Mirror Ash"). Unlike his regular world counterpart, Mirror Pikachu is a mischievous roughneck.

In *[Lights! Camera! Pika!](/wiki/XY078 "XY078")*, [Frank](/wiki/Frank "Frank") has multiple Pikachu who participate in his filmmaking endeavors.

In *[Hoopa and the Clash of Ages](/wiki/M18 "M18")*, multiple Pikachu were summoned by [Hoopa](/wiki/Hoopa_(anime) "Hoopa (anime)") as part of a prank on Ash and his Pikachu, though the prank failed. They were then returned to their original locations with the help of Ash's Pikachu, who coordinated them into Hoopa's portals.

In *[Battling at Full Volume!](/wiki/XY115 "XY115")*, [Jimmy](/wiki/Jimmy_(XY115) "Jimmy (XY115)")'s Pikachu, nicknamed Spike, was used in a battle against Ash's Pikachu. Since Ash was ill, [Serena](/wiki/Serena_(anime) "Serena (anime)") took his place, unbeknownst to Jimmy and Spike. However, the match was interrupted by Team Rocket, who captured Spike, though he was promptly saved.

Multiple Pikachu appeared in *[A Plethora of Pikachu!](/wiki/SM091 "SM091")* under the ownership of [Pikala](/wiki/Pikala "Pikala"). One of them is nicknamed Curly, while another one is [Shiny](/wiki/Shiny_Pok%C3%A9mon "Shiny Pokémon") and called the Boss. The Boss reappeared in a flashback in *[Beauty is Only Crystal Deep!](/wiki/SM114 "SM114")*. In *[Battle Royal 151!](/wiki/SM129 "SM129")*, another one of Pikala's Pikachu, nicknamed Bolt, competed in the [Battle Royal](/wiki/Battle_Royal "Battle Royal") round of the [Manalo Conference](/wiki/Manalo_Conference "Manalo Conference"). It defeated a [Magikarp](/wiki/Magikarp_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Magikarp (Pokémon)") and [Pyukumuku](/wiki/Pyukumuku_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pyukumuku (Pokémon)"), but ended up losing to [Jessie's Mimikyu](/wiki/Jessie%27s_Mimikyu "Jessie's Mimikyu").

Several Pikachu appeared in [*Gotta Catch a* What?!](/wiki/JN035 "JN035"). Several of them evolved into Raichu using the [Thunder Stones](/wiki/Thunder_Stone "Thunder Stone") they dug up.

A Pikachu owned by [Alternate World Ash](/wiki/Alternate_World_Ash "Alternate World Ash") appeared in *[Showdown at the Gates of Warp!](/wiki/JN090 "JN090")*.

#### Minor appearances

In *[Pokémon Emergency!](/wiki/EP002 "EP002")*, [Nurse Joy](/wiki/Nurse_Joy "Nurse Joy") had multiple Pikachu, all of whom helped Ash's own Pikachu defeat [Team Rocket](/wiki/Team_Rocket_trio "Team Rocket trio") and send them [blasting off](/wiki/Blasting_off "Blasting off") for the first time. They reappeared in a flashback in *[Pikachu's Goodbye](/wiki/EP039 "EP039")*.

Multiple Pikachu appeared in *[Battle Aboard the St. Anne](/wiki/EP015 "EP015")*.

In a flashback in *[Pokémon Double Trouble](/wiki/EP108 "EP108")*, [Travis](/wiki/Travis "Travis")'s mother [Luana](/wiki/Luana "Luana") temporarily mistook Ash for Travis when she saw Pikachu on Ash's shoulder, since Travis has a Pikachu of his own.

A Pikachu appeared in Brock's demonstration in *[A Bite to Remember](/wiki/AG011 "AG011")*, where it [evolved](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") into a [Raichu](/wiki/Raichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raichu (Pokémon)").

In *[Lights, Camerupt, Action!](/wiki/AG086 "AG086")*, a Pikachu appeared in two of [Elijah](/wiki/Elijah "Elijah")'s movies. [Red's Pikachu](/wiki/Red%27s_Pikachu_(Pocket_Monsters) "Red's Pikachu (Pocket Monsters)") was featured in a film that Ash and Gary were watching when they were younger. In a different movie that Ash and [his friends](/wiki/Ash%27s_friends "Ash's friends") were watching at the time the episode was taking place, another Pikachu helped [Plusle](/wiki/Plusle_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Plusle (Pokémon)") and [Minun](/wiki/Minun_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Minun (Pokémon)") on their mission to rescue Princess [Kirlia](/wiki/Kirlia_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Kirlia (Pokémon)") from the evil [Exploud](/wiki/Exploud_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Exploud (Pokémon)").

In *[Lucario and the Mystery of Mew](/wiki/M08 "M08")*, a Pikachu was one of [Mew](/wiki/Mew_(M08) "Mew (M08)")'s [transformations](/wiki/Transform_(move) "Transform (move)").

In *[Cooking up a Sweet Story!](/wiki/DP024 "DP024")*, [Abigail](/wiki/Abigail "Abigail")'s Pikachu, nicknamed Sugar, went missing prior to the events of the episode. As a result, Ash's Pikachu had to fill in for it so that its owner would have the confidence to win a cooking competition. At the end of the episode, Sugar returned, whereupon it was revealed to have evolved into a Raichu.

A Pikachu appeared in flashbacks in *[The Keystone Pops!](/wiki/DP056 "DP056")*, under the ownership of an [Aura Guardian](/wiki/Aura "Aura").

[Volkner](/wiki/Volkner "Volkner")'s Raichu was seen as a Pikachu during flashbacks of his childhood in *[Flint Sparks the Fire!](/wiki/DP165 "DP165")*.

A Pikachu appeared in [Clemont's demonstration](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") in *[To Catch a Pokémon Smuggler!](/wiki/XY012 "XY012")*, where it evolved into a Raichu using a [Thunder Stone](/wiki/Thunder_Stone "Thunder Stone").

A Pikachu appeared in *[Diancie and the Cocoon of Destruction](/wiki/M17 "M17")*, under the ownership of [Uschi](/wiki/Uschi "Uschi").

A Pikachu appeared in a fantasy in *[Now You See Them, Now You Don't!](/wiki/SM045 "SM045")*.

A Pikachu appeared in a fantasy in *[Showering the World with Love!](/wiki/SM079 "SM079")*.

In *[Securing the Future!](/wiki/SM090 "SM090")*, a [Trainer](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer "Pokémon Trainer")'s Pikachu joined the rest of [Alola](/wiki/Alola "Alola") in showering [Necrozma](/wiki/Necrozma_(anime) "Necrozma (anime)") with light so it could return to its [normal form](/wiki/List_of_Pok%C3%A9mon_with_form_differences#Necrozma "List of Pokémon with form differences").

In *[Battle Royal 151!](/wiki/SM129 "SM129")*, a Trainer's Pikachu competed in the [Battle Royal](/wiki/Battle_Royal "Battle Royal") preliminary round of the [Manalo Conference](/wiki/Manalo_Conference "Manalo Conference") but ended up losing.

Multiple Pikachu appeared in *[Distant Blue Sky!](/wiki/JNS05 "JNS05")*.

Multiple Pikachu wearing hats matching [Captain Pikachu](/wiki/Captain_Pikachu "Captain Pikachu")'s hat appeared in a bonus scene aired with [HZ042](/wiki/HZ042 "HZ042"). This scene was not dubbed into English.

### Pokémon Mystery Dungeon Animated Shorts

A female appeared in the [Pokémon Mystery Dungeon Animated Shorts](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Mystery_Dungeon_Animated_Shorts "Pokémon Mystery Dungeon Animated Shorts") as the protagonist along with an [Oshawott](/wiki/Oshawott_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Oshawott (Pokémon)").

### Pokémon Origins

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/f/fd/Red_Pikachu_PO.png/250px-Red_Pikachu_PO.png)](/wiki/File:Red_Pikachu_PO.png)Pikachu in [Pokémon Origins](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Origins "Pokémon Origins")

#### [Red's Pikachu](/wiki/Red%27s_Pikachu "Red's Pikachu")

[Red](/wiki/Red_(Origins) "Red (Origins)") [caught](/wiki/Caught_Pok%C3%A9mon "Caught Pokémon") a Pikachu in *[File 4: Charizard](/wiki/PO04 "PO04")*.

#### Other

In *[File 1: Red](/wiki/PO01 "PO01")*, a Pikachu was one of the Pokémon seen in [Professor Oak](/wiki/Professor_Oak "Professor Oak")'s introduction.

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/d/da/Red_Pikachu_PG.png/250px-Red_Pikachu_PG.png)](/wiki/File:Red_Pikachu_PG.png)Pikachu in [Pokémon Generations](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Generations "Pokémon Generations")

### Pokémon Generations

#### [Red's Pikachu](/wiki/Red%27s_Pikachu "Red's Pikachu")

[Red](/wiki/Red_(game) "Red (game)") caught a Pikachu in [Viridian Forest](/wiki/Viridian_Forest "Viridian Forest") in *[The Adventure](/wiki/PG01 "PG01")*. He then proceeded to travel though multiple [regions](/wiki/Region "Region") with it, facing many strong opponents along the way.

### Pokémon Masters Animated Trailer

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/3/3e/Scottie_Pikachu_Masters_Trailer.png/250px-Scottie_Pikachu_Masters_Trailer.png)](/wiki/File:Scottie_Pikachu_Masters_Trailer.png)Pikachu in the Pokémon Masters Animated Trailer

Two Pikachu appeared in the [Pokémon Masters Animated Trailer](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Masters_Animated_Trailer "Pokémon Masters Animated Trailer"), with one under the ownership of [Scottie](/wiki/Scottie "Scottie") and the other under the ownership of [Bettie](/wiki/Bettie "Bettie"). Scottie's Pikachu participated in a [battle](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_battle "Pokémon battle") against [Erika](/wiki/Erika_(Masters) "Erika (Masters)"), a [Beauty](/wiki/Beauty_(Trainer_class) "Beauty (Trainer class)"), and a [Lass](/wiki/Lass_(Trainer_class) "Lass (Trainer class)"); while Bettie's Pikachu participated in a battle against [Skyla](/wiki/Skyla_(Masters) "Skyla (Masters)"), [Flint](/wiki/Flint_(Masters) "Flint (Masters)"), and [Acerola](/wiki/Acerola_(Masters) "Acerola (Masters)").

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/1/1c/Schoolboy_Schoolgirl_Youngster_Twilight_Wings.png/250px-Schoolboy_Schoolgirl_Youngster_Twilight_Wings.png)](/wiki/File:Schoolboy_Schoolgirl_Youngster_Twilight_Wings.png)Pikachu in [Pokémon: Twilight Wings](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon:_Twilight_Wings "Pokémon: Twilight Wings")

### Pokémon: Twilight Wings

A Pikachu appeared in *[Letter](/wiki/TW01 "TW01")*.

A Pikachu appeared in *[Buddy](/wiki/TW03 "TW03")*, under the ownership of a [Schoolboy](/wiki/Schoolboy_(Trainer_class) "Schoolboy (Trainer class)").

### POKÉTOON

A Pikachu appeared in *[The Pancham Who Wants to Be a Hero](/wiki/PT02 "PT02")*, where it was trapped in a cave.

In *[Blossom's Dream](/wiki/PT03 "PT03")*, [Blossom](/wiki/Blossom_(POK%C3%89TOON) "Blossom (POKÉTOON)") attempted to catch a Pikachu, but failed.

A Pikachu appeared in [PT15](/wiki/PT15 "PT15"), as one of the many Pokémon angered by [Akira](/wiki/Akira "Akira")'s [Capsakid](/wiki/Capsakid_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Capsakid (Pokémon)")'s spicy bite.

### GOTCHA!

A Pikachu appeared in [GOTCHA!](/wiki/GOTCHA! "GOTCHA!"), under the ownership of a boy.

### Pokémon Evolutions

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/e/e4/Elaine_Pikachu_Evolutions.png/250px-Elaine_Pikachu_Evolutions.png)](/wiki/File:Elaine_Pikachu_Evolutions.png)Pikachu in Pokémon Evolutions

Throughout [Pokémon Evolutions](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Evolutions "Pokémon Evolutions"), two Pikachu wearing the hats of the [player characters](/wiki/Player_character "Player character") from the [generation](/wiki/Generation "Generation") featured in the episode appeared.

- In *[The Champion](/wiki/PE01 "PE01")*, they wore [Victor](/wiki/Victor_(game) "Victor (game)") and [Gloria](/wiki/Gloria_(game) "Gloria (game)")'s hats and appeared in [Wyndon Stadium](/wiki/Wyndon_Stadium "Wyndon Stadium"), cheering on the [battle](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_battle "Pokémon battle") between Victor and [Leon](/wiki/Leon "Leon").
- In *[The Eclipse](/wiki/PE02 "PE02")*, they wore [Elio](/wiki/Elio_(game) "Elio (game)") and [Selene](/wiki/Selene_(game) "Selene (game)")'s hats and appeared playing with some children.
- In *[The Visionary](/wiki/PE03 "PE03")*, they wore [Calem](/wiki/Calem_(game) "Calem (game)") and [Serena](/wiki/Serena_(game) "Serena (game)")'s hats and appeared near [Lysandre](/wiki/Lysandre "Lysandre") and [Diantha](/wiki/Diantha "Diantha") while they were having a conversation.
- In *[The Plan](/wiki/PE04 "PE04")*, they wore [Hilbert](/wiki/Hilbert_(game) "Hilbert (game)") and [Hilda](/wiki/Hilda_(game) "Hilda (game)")'s hats and appeared under the ownership of a Trainer who was listening to [Ghetsis](/wiki/Ghetsis "Ghetsis")'s speech in [Accumula Town](/wiki/Accumula_Town "Accumula Town").
- In *[The Rival](/wiki/PE05 "PE05")*, they wore [Lucas](/wiki/Lucas_(game) "Lucas (game)") and [Dawn](/wiki/Dawn_(game) "Dawn (game)")'s hats and appeared in the reception hall of the [Battle Tower](/wiki/Battle_Tower_(Sinnoh) "Battle Tower (Sinnoh)").
- In *[The Wish](/wiki/PE06 "PE06")*, they wore [Brendan](/wiki/Brendan_(game) "Brendan (game)") and [May](/wiki/May_(game) "May (game)")'s hats and appeared in [Pacifidlog Town](/wiki/Pacifidlog_Town "Pacifidlog Town"), watching the battle between May's [Mega](/wiki/Mega_Evolution "Mega Evolution") [Rayquaza](/wiki/Rayquaza_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Rayquaza (Pokémon)") and [Zinnia](/wiki/Zinnia "Zinnia")'s Mega [Salamence](/wiki/Salamence_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Salamence (Pokémon)").
- In *[The Show](/wiki/PE07 "PE07")*, they wore [Ethan](/wiki/Ethan_(game) "Ethan (game)") and [Lyra](/wiki/Lyra_(game) "Lyra (game)")'s hats and appeared under the ownership of [Trainers](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer "Pokémon Trainer") watching the [Kimono Girls](/wiki/Kimono_Girl "Kimono Girl")' performance in [Ecruteak City](/wiki/Ecruteak_City "Ecruteak City").
- In *[The Discovery](/wiki/PE08 "PE08")*, they wore [Chase](/wiki/Chase_(game) "Chase (game)") and [Elaine](/wiki/Elaine_(game) "Elaine (game)")'s hats and appeared in [Pallet Town](/wiki/Pallet_Town "Pallet Town").


In *The Eclipse*, a Pikachu was briefly seen in a flashback, frozen in [Lusamine](/wiki/Lusamine "Lusamine")'s trophy room.

In *The Discovery*, a female [partner](/wiki/Partner_Pok%C3%A9mon_(Let%27s_Go,_Pikachu!_and_Let%27s_Go,_Eevee!) "Partner Pokémon (Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!)") Pikachu wearing the Sportswear appeared under the ownership of Elaine.

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/b/bc/Pikachu_BBS.png/250px-Pikachu_BBS.png)](/wiki/File:Pikachu_BBS.png)Pikachu in *Bidoof's Big Stand*[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/2/27/Pikachu_and_Ash_ARIT.png/250px-Pikachu_and_Ash_ARIT.png)](/wiki/File:Pikachu_and_Ash_ARIT.png)Pikachu in *A Ripple in Time*

### Bidoof's Big Stand

A Pikachu appeared in the intro to *[Bidoof's Big Stand](/wiki/Bidoof%27s_Big_Stand "Bidoof's Big Stand")*

### A Ripple in Time

A Pikachu based on [Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu") has a prominent role in *[A Ripple in Time](/wiki/A_Ripple_in_Time "A Ripple in Time")* along with an alternate version of [Ash](/wiki/Ash_Ketchum_(A_Ripple_in_Time) "Ash Ketchum (A Ripple in Time)").

### Pokémon Concierge

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/8/80/Pok%C3%A9mon_Concierge_Nao_Pikachu.png/250px-Pok%C3%A9mon_Concierge_Nao_Pikachu.png)](/wiki/File:Pok%C3%A9mon_Concierge_Nao_Pikachu.png)Nao's Pikachu in Pokémon Concierge

Multiple Pikachu, twelve in total, appear in the [Pokémon Concierge](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Concierge "Pokémon Concierge") episode *[Welcome to the Pokémon Resort!](/wiki/PC04 "PC04")* as guests at [the titular resort](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Resort "Pokémon Resort"). One of these Pikachu, belonging to [Nao](/wiki/Nao "Nao"), has a quiet and asocial personality unlike most other Pikachu, and thus Nao asks for [Haru](/wiki/Haru "Haru")'s help in trying to make it louder and more "Pikachu-like". Over their many failed attempts, however, they eventually come to the realization that Nao's Pikachu does not need to be forced into a specific "Pikachu-like" role, and that it should be allowed to be itself. Once Nao realizes this, his bond with Pikachu grows closer and Pikachu becomes happier.

Multiple Pikachu made cameo appearances in the following episodes.

### The Journey of One Dream

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/d/d2/Dominic_Pikachu_SM.png/250px-Dominic_Pikachu_SM.png)](/wiki/File:Dominic_Pikachu_SM.png)Pikachu in The Journey of One Dream

Pikachu plays an important role in [The Journey of One Dream](/wiki/The_Journey_of_One_Dream "The Journey of One Dream") as one of the many Pokémon featured in Dominic's games. In the short's final [battle](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_battle "Pokémon battle"), Pikachu is used by [Dominic](/wiki/Dominic "Dominic") in the final battle against a [Mewtwo](/wiki/Mewtwo_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Mewtwo (Pokémon)").

### Biri-Biri

[Anna](/wiki/Juliana_(game) "Juliana (game)")'s Pikachu briefly appeared as a silhouette in the music video for [Biri-Biri](/wiki/Biri-Biri "Biri-Biri").

## In the manga

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/e/e8/Pika.png/200px-Pika.png)](/wiki/File:Pika.png)Pikachu in [Pokémon Adventures](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Adventures "Pokémon Adventures")

### Pokémon Adventures

#### Major appearances

##### [Pika](/wiki/Pika "Pika")

Pikachu debuted in *[Wanted: Pikachu!](/wiki/PS004 "PS004")*, where it was stealing food in [Pewter City](/wiki/Pewter_City "Pewter City"). The townsfolk kept chasing him around, but ended up getting shocked by him. [Red](/wiki/Red_(Adventures) "Red (Adventures)") arrived and caught him, ending the chaos. Although extremely disobedient at first, even prone to regularly electrocuting his owner, the two eventually got over this enmity, and this Pikachu, nicknamed "Pika", became one of his most loyal and trusted fighters, being used in almost every major [battle](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_battle "Pokémon battle") Red has had.

Pika served on [Yellow](/wiki/Yellow_(Adventures) "Yellow (Adventures)")'s team while Red missing during this chapter, before being returned to him. During his time with Yellow, Pika learned how to [Surf](/wiki/Surf_(move) "Surf (move)").

##### [Chuchu](/wiki/Chuchu "Chuchu")

In *[Tyranitar War](/wiki/PS111 "PS111")*, Yellow was revealed to have obtained her own Pikachu, nicknamed Chuchu. While staying at the [Pokémon Day-Care](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Day-Care "Pokémon Day-Care") with Pika, the two Pikachu ended up producing a [Pokémon Egg](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Egg "Pokémon Egg"), which later hatched into [Gold](/wiki/Gold_(Adventures) "Gold (Adventures)")'s [Pichu](/wiki/Pichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pichu (Pokémon)"), [Pibu](/wiki/Pibu "Pibu").

##### [Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu")

Five Pikachu appeared in *[Salamence Evolves](/wiki/PS599 "PS599")*, where they each wear the five costumes available for the [Cosplay Pikachu](/wiki/Cosplay_Pikachu "Cosplay Pikachu"): Pikachu Rock Star, Pikachu Belle, Pikachu Pop Star, Pikachu Ph.D., and Pikachu Libre. Lisia revealed to [Chaz](/wiki/Chaz_(game) "Chaz (game)") that [Ruby](/wiki/Ruby_(Adventures) "Ruby (Adventures)") created the concept of Pokémon wearing costumes for [Contests](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Contest "Pokémon Contest"). Noticeably, some of these Cosplay Pikachu were male. They reappeared in a flashback in *[Slowbro Has an Idea](/wiki/PS603 "PS603")*.

##### Other

[Hau](/wiki/Hau "Hau")'s Pichu evolved into Pikachu prior to *[Shock!! Father in the Pendant!](/wiki/PASM32 "PASM32")*.

A Pikachu appeared in *[Gulp!! Gulp Pokémon](/wiki/PASS11 "PASS11")*, having been swallowed by a [Cramorant](/wiki/Cramorant_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Cramorant (Pokémon)"). It reappeared in *[Rumble!! The Darkest Day Returns](/wiki/PASS40 "PASS40")* and *[Shing!! Final Form](/wiki/PASS41 "PASS41")*, where it had [Gigantamaxed](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax") due to the [Darkest Day](/wiki/Darkest_Day "Darkest Day").

A female Pikachu appeared in *[Speeding! Paldea's Prince of Speed!!](/wiki/PASV01 "PASV01")*, under [Scarlet](/wiki/Scarlet_Koito "Scarlet Koito")'s ownership. She was shown to be able to [Terastallize](/wiki/Terastal_phenomenon "Terastal phenomenon") into a [Flying](/wiki/Flying_(type) "Flying (type)") type.

#### Minor appearances

A Pikachu appeared as a silhouette when [Green](/wiki/Green_(Adventures) "Green (Adventures)") talked about [Mew](/wiki/Mew_(Adventures) "Mew (Adventures)") in *[The Jynx Jinx](/wiki/PS017 "PS017")*.

In *[Just a Spearow Carrier](/wiki/PS039 "PS039")*, a Pikachu appeared with its [Trainer](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer "Pokémon Trainer") at [Indigo Plateau](/wiki/Indigo_Plateau "Indigo Plateau"). It noticeably had a flower by its ear.

A Pikachu appeared in *[The Kindest Tentacruel](/wiki/PS058 "PS058")* as one of the [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_(species) "Pokémon (species)") in Yellow's fantasy about [Evolution](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") via [Evolution stone](/wiki/Evolution_stone "Evolution stone").

A Pikachu appeared in *[Can You Diglett?](/wiki/PS067 "PS067")* as a silhouette when [Agatha](/wiki/Agatha "Agatha") and [Lorelei](/wiki/Lorelei "Lorelei") explained to Red about how Pokémon and people can't coexist with each other.

A Pikachu appeared in a fantasy of [Professor Elm](/wiki/Professor_Elm "Professor Elm")'s explanation of [Pokémon Eggs](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Egg "Pokémon Egg") in *[Teddiursa's Picnic](/wiki/PS101 "PS101")*.

A Pikachu appeared in *[Ursaring Major](/wiki/PS102 "PS102")* in a fantasy of Silver when he explained the vital points of Pokémon.

Two Pikachu owned by a pair of [Poké Fans](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9_Fan_(Trainer_class) "Poké Fan (Trainer class)") appeared in *[Oh, It's Ho-Oh!](/wiki/PS153 "PS153")*, going towards Indigo Plateau.

A Pikachu appeared in a fantasy in *[Pokémon Ranger the Comic: Double Mission Episode One](/wiki/PR1 "PR1")*.

A Pikachu appeared in *[Suffering Psyduck](/wiki/PS367 "PS367")*.

Multiple Pikachu were among the [Electric-type](/wiki/Electric_(type) "Electric (type)") Pokémon that charged the [Prism Tower](/wiki/Prism_Tower "Prism Tower") during a blackout in *[Pangoro Poses a Problem](/wiki/PS562 "PS562")*.

A Trainer's Pikachu appeared in *[The Decision and the Tournament of Six](/wiki/PASM04 "PASM04")*.

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/7/74/Red_Pikachu_PM.png/200px-Red_Pikachu_PM.png)](/wiki/File:Red_Pikachu_PM.png)Pikachu in [Pokémon Pocket Monsters](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Pocket_Monsters "Pokémon Pocket Monsters")

### Pokémon Pocket Monsters

*Main article: [Red's Pikachu (Pocket Monsters)](/wiki/Red%27s_Pikachu_(Pocket_Monsters) "Red's Pikachu (Pocket Monsters)")*

Pikachu is one of the main characters in [Pokémon Pocket Monsters](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Pocket_Monsters "Pokémon Pocket Monsters") and its sequels. It was the second Pokémon that [Red](/wiki/Red_(Pocket_Monsters) "Red (Pocket Monsters)") [caught](/wiki/Caught_Pok%C3%A9mon "Caught Pokémon") on his own, but the first he kept.

Pikachu is one of the few Pokémon in the manga that cannot [speak human language](/wiki/Talking_Pok%C3%A9mon "Talking Pokémon"). He is the cousin of [Clefairy](/wiki/Red%27s_Clefairy "Red's Clefairy") and is considered the smarter of the two.

In *[Clefairy Evolves... At Last?!](/wiki/PM023 "PM023")*, Pikachu evolved into [Raichu](/wiki/Raichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raichu (Pokémon)"), but he has since [devolved](/wiki/Devolution "Devolution") back into a Pikachu.

[Yellow](/wiki/Yellow_(Pocket_Monsters) "Yellow (Pocket Monsters)") has a Pikachu of his own, which debuted in *[The Fake Pikachu!!](/wiki/PM044 "PM044")*.

### The Electric Tale of Pikachu

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/a/aa/Ash_Pikachu_EToP.png/200px-Ash_Pikachu_EToP.png)](/wiki/File:Ash_Pikachu_EToP.png)Pikachu in [The Electric Tale of Pikachu](/wiki/The_Electric_Tale_of_Pikachu "The Electric Tale of Pikachu")

*Main article: [Ash's Pikachu (EToP)](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu_(EToP) "Ash's Pikachu (EToP)")*

Ash's Pikachu is a main character in [The Electric Tale of Pikachu](/wiki/The_Electric_Tale_of_Pikachu "The Electric Tale of Pikachu"), an adaptation of *[Pokémon the Series](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series "Pokémon the Series")*. Ash discovered it under the floorboards at his home, where it was chewing on wires to eat the electricity. After catching it, Ash decided to make it his [first Pokémon](/wiki/List_of_characters%27_first_Pok%C3%A9mon "List of characters' first Pokémon").

[Sparky](/wiki/Sparky "Sparky"), [Ritchie](/wiki/Ritchie "Ritchie")'s Pikachu, first appeared in *[Welcome to the Big Leagues](/wiki/ET12 "ET12")*. In this manga, its [nickname](/wiki/Nickname "Nickname") is translated as Chuchino.
