### Magical Pokémon Journey and Pokémon Chamo-Chamo ☆ Pretty ♪

*Main article: [Pikachu (MPJ)](/wiki/Pikachu_(MPJ) "Pikachu (MPJ)")*

Pikachu is a main character in [Magical Pokémon Journey](/wiki/Magical_Pok%C3%A9mon_Journey "Magical Pokémon Journey"). He is the first Pokémon befriended by [Hazel](/wiki/Hazel "Hazel"), debuting in *[How Do You Do, Pikachu?](/wiki/PPP01 "PPP01")*. Pikachu is one of the few Pokémon in Magical Pokémon Journey that cannot speak human language, although the others seem to understand him. He is described as rather scatterbrained at times. Pikachu returns as a main character in [Pokémon Chamo-Chamo ☆ Pretty ♪](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Chamo-Chamo_%E2%98%86_Pretty_%E2%99%AA "Pokémon Chamo-Chamo ☆ Pretty ♪"), the sequel to the Magical Pokémon Journey series.

[Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu") also makes a cameo appearance in bonus chapters at the end of every volume of the Magical Pokémon Journey manga.

### Pokémon Zensho

[Satoshi](/wiki/Satoshi_(Zensho) "Satoshi (Zensho)") caught a Pikachu in [PZ02](/wiki/PZ02 "PZ02"). It later evolved into a Raichu in [PZ10](/wiki/PZ10 "PZ10").

### Pokémon: Yeah! I Got Pokémon!

*Main article: [Shu's Pikachu](/wiki/Shu%27s_Pikachu "Shu's Pikachu")*

[Shu](/wiki/Shu "Shu")'s first Pokémon was a Pikachu that he found abandoned in the forest.

A female Pikachu appeared in [GDZ40](/wiki/GDZ40 "GDZ40"), under the ownership of a male Trainer.

A Pikachu appeared in [GDZ61](/wiki/GDZ61 "GDZ61").

### How I Became a Pokémon Card

A story called *[Akari and Pikachu's Birthday](/wiki/PW38 "PW38")* is featured in the [final volume](/wiki/How_I_Became_a_Pok%C3%A9mon_Card_volume_6 "How I Became a Pokémon Card volume 6") of [How I Became a Pokémon Card](/wiki/How_I_Became_a_Pok%C3%A9mon_Card "How I Became a Pokémon Card").

### Pokémon Gold & Silver: The Golden Boys

*Main article: [Gold's Pikachu](/wiki/Gold%27s_Pikachu "Gold's Pikachu")*

[Gold](/wiki/Gold_(Golden_Boys) "Gold (Golden Boys)") owns a Pikachu, which was later revealed to have originally belonged to [Eusine](/wiki/Eusine "Eusine") before he sent it [back in time](/wiki/Time_travel "Time travel") with [Bill](/wiki/Bill "Bill")'s [Time Capsule](/wiki/Time_Capsule "Time Capsule").

### Pokémon Newspaper Strip

[Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu") was the main character in the short-lived [Pokémon Newspaper Strip](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Newspaper_Strip "Pokémon Newspaper Strip").

### Ash & Pikachu

*Main article: [Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu")*

[Ash](/wiki/Ash_Ketchum "Ash Ketchum")'s Pikachu is a starring character in [Ash & Pikachu](/wiki/Ash_%26_Pikachu "Ash &amp; Pikachu"), a manga adaptation of *[Pokémon the Series](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series "Pokémon the Series")* based on Ash's adventures in [Johto](/wiki/Johto "Johto"), [Hoenn](/wiki/Hoenn "Hoenn"), and [Battle Frontier](/wiki/Battle_Frontier_(Generation_III) "Battle Frontier (Generation III)").

### Pokémon Battle Frontier

A Pikachu appeared in *[Aim for the Top!](/wiki/CBF2 "CBF2")*.

A Pikachu appeared in *[Battle Between Student and Master!](/wiki/CBF3 "CBF3")*.

A Pikachu appeared in *[Introducing the Frontier Brains!](/wiki/CBF4 "CBF4")*, where it was seen in a picture taken by [Anabel](/wiki/Anabel "Anabel").

### Pocket Monsters Diamond & Pearl

*Main article: [Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu")*

Ash's Pikachu appeared in [Pocket Monsters Diamond & Pearl](/wiki/Pocket_Monsters_Diamond_%26_Pearl_(Sakai_Takayuki) "Pocket Monsters Diamond &amp; Pearl (Sakai Takayuki)").

### Pokémon Battrio: Aim to be Battrio Master!

A Pikachu appeared in [ABM02](/wiki/ABM02 "ABM02").

### Pocket Monsters HeartGold & SoulSilver Go! Go! Pokéathlon

A Pikachu appeared in [Pocket Monsters HeartGold & SoulSilver Go! Go! Pokéathlon](/wiki/Pocket_Monsters_HeartGold_%26_SoulSilver_Go!_Go!_Pok%C3%A9athlon "Pocket Monsters HeartGold &amp; SoulSilver Go! Go! Pokéathlon").

### Pocket Monsters HGSS Jō's Big Adventure

*Main article: [Red's Pikachu](/wiki/Red%27s_Pikachu "Red's Pikachu")*

Red's Pikachu debuted as a silhouette alongside its Trainer in [JBA5](/wiki/JBA5 "JBA5"). It then physically debuted in [JBA6](/wiki/JBA6 "JBA6").

### Pokémon + Nobunaga's Ambition ~ Ranse's Color Picture Scroll ~

A Pikachu appeared in [PNA3](/wiki/PNA3 "PNA3"), under the ownership of [Hanbei](/wiki/Hanbei "Hanbei").

### Pokémon Horizon

A Trainer's Pikachu appeared in a fantasy in *[Journey to a New Horizon](/wiki/HOR01 "HOR01")*.

A Pikachu appeared in a fantasy in *[The Power of Teamwork](/wiki/HOR02 "HOR02")*.

### Pokémon Journeys

*Main article: [Ash's Pikachu](/wiki/Ash%27s_Pikachu "Ash's Pikachu")*

Ash's Pikachu appeared in [Pokémon Journeys](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Journeys_(manga) "Pokémon Journeys (manga)").

## In the TCG

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/7/76/PikachuBaseSet58.jpg/200px-PikachuBaseSet58.jpg)](/wiki/File:PikachuBaseSet58.jpg)Pikachu in the TCG[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/1/1a/GBML_Bronze_Pikachu_Coin.png/100px-GBML_Bronze_Pikachu_Coin.png)](/wiki/File:GBML_Bronze_Pikachu_Coin.png)Pikachu TCG Coin

*Main article: [Pikachu (TCG)](/wiki/Pikachu_(TCG) "Pikachu (TCG)")*

Pikachu manages to find its way into many of the expansions, causing there to be 221 known non-reprint [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_(species) "Pokémon (species)") cards featuring it. There are also various [Trainer](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer "Pokémon Trainer") cards with Pikachu in the card's artwork too, including the very sought-after tournament promos such as [No.1 Trainer](/wiki/No.1_Trainer_(Tournament_promo) "No.1 Trainer (Tournament promo)").

The first Pikachu that appeared in the TCG was [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Base_Set_58) "Pikachu (Base Set 58)") in the [Base Set](/wiki/Base_Set_(TCG) "Base Set (TCG)") (which was reprinted in [Base Set 2](/wiki/Base_Set_2_(TCG) "Base Set 2 (TCG)") and in [POP Series 2](/wiki/POP_Series_2_(TCG) "POP Series 2 (TCG)") with different artwork). It was followed in [the next set](/wiki/Jungle_(TCG) "Jungle (TCG)") by another [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Jungle_60) "Pikachu (Jungle 60)") (which was also reprinted in the [Legendary Collection](/wiki/Legendary_Collection_(TCG) "Legendary Collection (TCG)")). The third Pikachu card released was the first-ever English [promo card](/wiki/Wizards_Black_Star_Promos_(TCG) "Wizards Black Star Promos (TCG)"), [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Wizards_Promo_1) "Pikachu (Wizards Promo 1)"). The original Base Set Pikachu was also released as a special promotional card at [E3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E3 "wp:E3").

In the early days of the TCG, several notable errors were made regarding the Pikachu cards. The Base Set Pikachu was released with altered artwork at first, depicting it with red cheeks, instead of the original yellow. While this matched [Ken Sugimori](/wiki/Ken_Sugimori "Ken Sugimori")'s artwork, the yellow color on its cheeks was intentional by the artist, who depicted Pikachu using [ThunderShock](/wiki/ThunderShock_(move) "ThunderShock (move)"). This error appeared in both the E3 promotional version and the normal Base Set release. A second error was made in the production of Jungle set booster packs, which, instead of the Jungle set's Pikachu, sometimes contained first edition versions of the Pikachu that was released as a promo card.

Pikachu has also been featured on a number of TCG [coins](/wiki/Coin_(TCG) "Coin (TCG)"), with one being included with the English and European languages-only [Base Set 2](/wiki/Base_Set_2_(TCG) "Base Set 2 (TCG)"). This coin was also featured in the [Pokémon Play It!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Play_It! "Pokémon Play It!") PC game. A Pikachu coin is also featured in the [Game Boy](/wiki/Game_Boy "Game Boy") game [Pokémon Trading Card Game](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trading_Card_Game_(video_game) "Pokémon Trading Card Game (video game)"), and is one of the coins that can be obtained in [Pokémon Trading Card Game 2: The Invasion of Team GR!](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trading_Card_Game_2:_The_Invasion_of_Team_GR! "Pokémon Trading Card Game 2: The Invasion of Team GR!"), where it is given by [Isaac](/wiki/Isaac_(TCG_GB) "Isaac (TCG GB)") to winners of the [Lightning Club](/wiki/Lightning_Club "Lightning Club").

## In the TFG

Two Pikachu figures have been released.

- [Next Quest](/wiki/Next_Quest_(TFG) "Next Quest (TFG)"): [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Next_Quest_18) "Pikachu (Next Quest 18)")
- [Black Star Promo](/wiki/Black_Star_Promo_(TFG) "Black Star Promo (TFG)"): [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Black_Star_Promo_8) "Pikachu (Black Star Promo 8)")


## Other appearances

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/6/63/Pokk%C3%A9n_Pikachu_Libre.png/200px-Pokk%C3%A9n_Pikachu_Libre.png)](/wiki/File:Pokk%C3%A9n_Pikachu_Libre.png)Pikachu Libre in Pokkén Tournament[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/0/03/UNITE_Pikachu.png/200px-UNITE_Pikachu.png)](/wiki/File:UNITE_Pikachu.png)Pikachu in Pokémon UNITE

### [Pokkén Tournament](/wiki/Pokk%C3%A9n_Tournament "Pokkén Tournament")

*Main article: [Pikachu (Pokkén)](/wiki/Pikachu_(Pokk%C3%A9n) "Pikachu (Pokkén)")*

*Main article: [Pikachu Libre (Pokkén)](/wiki/Pikachu_Libre_(Pokk%C3%A9n) "Pikachu Libre (Pokkén)")*

[Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Pokk%C3%A9n) "Pikachu (Pokkén)") is a Battle Pokémon in Pokkén Tournament. His moveset includes electrical attacks the species uses in the main games, including [Thunderbolt](/wiki/Thunderbolt_(move) "Thunderbolt (move)"), [Electro Ball](/wiki/Electro_Ball_(move) "Electro Ball (move)"), and [Thunder](/wiki/Thunder_(move) "Thunder (move)"). During Synergy Burst, he can use the Burst Attack Volt Shock Fist. Several of his attacks and victory poses are directly taken from [Heihachi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heihachi_Mishima "wp:Heihachi Mishima") and [Kazuya Mishima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazuya_Mishima "wp:Kazuya Mishima")'s movesets from the *[Tekken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tekken "wp:Tekken")* series.

*Pikachu overwhelms opponents with powerful electric shocks and quick movements.*[[5]](#cite_note-5)

[Pikachu Libre](/wiki/Pikachu_Libre_(Pokk%C3%A9n) "Pikachu Libre (Pokkén)") was first announced alongside the [Wii U](/wiki/Wii_U "Wii U") port of the game. She was later released as an additional fighter on the original arcade version.

*Pikachu Libre, a wrestling idol, is a small masked fighter overflowing with fighting spirit.*

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/7/76/Pikachu_Eliana_meet.png/175px-Pikachu_Eliana_meet.png)](/wiki/File:Pikachu_Eliana_meet.png)Pikachu and Eliana in The Strength of the Lightning

### [Super Smash Bros.](/wiki/Super_Smash_Bros. "Super Smash Bros.")

*Main article: [Pikachu (Super Smash Bros.)](/wiki/Pikachu_(Super_Smash_Bros.) "Pikachu (Super Smash Bros.)")*

### [Detective Pikachu](/wiki/Detective_Pikachu_(video_game) "Detective Pikachu (video game)")

Detective Pikachu stars a [Pikachu](/wiki/Detective_Pikachu_(character) "Detective Pikachu (character)") that is [capable of speaking the human language](/wiki/Talking_Pok%C3%A9mon "Talking Pokémon"), but only the player character, [Tim Goodman](/wiki/Tim_Goodman "Tim Goodman"), is capable of hearing him. Much like [Meowth](/wiki/Meowth_(Team_Rocket) "Meowth (Team Rocket)") from *[Pokémon the Series](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series "Pokémon the Series")*, he is incapable of using [moves](/wiki/Move "Move") that most Pikachu are capable of using. This Pikachu also really loves to drink coffee. Early on in the game, a regular Pikachu also briefly appears.

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/5/5f/Celestial_cover.png/175px-Celestial_cover.png)](/wiki/File:Celestial_cover.png)Single cover for [Celestial](/wiki/Celestial "Celestial")

### [Pokémon UNITE](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_UNITE "Pokémon UNITE")

*Main article: [Pikachu (UNITE)](/wiki/Pikachu_(UNITE) "Pikachu (UNITE)")*

Pikachu is playable after obtaining its [Unite license](/wiki/Unite_license "Unite license"). It is a ranged attacker.

### [The Strength of the Lightning](/wiki/The_Strength_of_the_Lightning "The Strength of the Lightning")

The Brazilian music video [The Strength of the Lightning](/wiki/The_Strength_of_the_Lightning "The Strength of the Lightning") features a stop motion animated Pikachu. This Pikachu is also shown later as a live action actor with a full-body costume.

### Celestial

Pikachu appeared in the music video for [Celestial](/wiki/Celestial "Celestial"). It is also featured in the single cover for the song.

## Trivia

- Pikachu and its evolved form both share their [category](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_category "Pokémon category") with [Rattata](/wiki/Rattata_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Rattata (Pokémon)"), [Raticate](/wiki/Raticate_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raticate (Pokémon)"), [Sandshrew](/wiki/Sandshrew_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Sandshrew (Pokémon)"), [Sandslash](/wiki/Sandslash_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Sandslash (Pokémon)"), [Pawmi](/wiki/Pawmi_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pawmi (Pokémon)"), and [Pawmo](/wiki/Pawmo_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pawmo (Pokémon)"). They are all known as the Mouse [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_(species) "Pokémon (species)"), though Rattata and Raticate were originally called the Rat Pokémon prior to [Generation III](/wiki/Generation_III "Generation III").
- [Veronica Taylor](/wiki/Veronica_Taylor "Veronica Taylor") has said Pikachu is her favorite [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_(species) "Pokémon (species)"). [Sarah Natochenny](/wiki/Sarah_Natochenny "Sarah Natochenny") has as well, shared with [Mew](/wiki/Mew_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Mew (Pokémon)").
- In [Generation VIII](/wiki/Generation_VIII "Generation VIII") onwards, Pikachu is capable of learning [Surf](/wiki/Surf_(move) "Surf (move)") via [TM](/wiki/TM "TM"), when it was previously only obtainable through special means.
- Pikachu is the only Pokémon that serves as the [player's first Pokémon](/wiki/List_of_the_player%27s_first_Pok%C3%A9mon "List of the player's first Pokémon") in the [core series](/wiki/Core_series "Core series") games that has a pre-evolved form. However, [Pichu](/wiki/Pichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Pichu (Pokémon)") (its pre-evolved form) does not exist in either game in which it fills this role.
- Pikachu has the most [event](/wiki/Event_Pok%C3%A9mon "Event Pokémon")-exclusive [moves](/wiki/Move "Move") available to it.
- Pikachu is the only Pokémon with two exclusive [Z-Moves](/wiki/Z-Move "Z-Move").
- Pikachu and [Eevee](/wiki/Eevee_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Eevee (Pokémon)") are the only non-fully evolved Pokémon with exclusive Z-Moves.
- The Pikachu family is the only evolutionary family to have access to [Mega Evolution](/wiki/Mega_Evolution "Mega Evolution"), exclusive Z-Moves, and [Gigantamax](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax").
- When starting a [new game](/wiki/New_game "New game") in [Pokémon Yellow](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version"), Pikachu's front sprite appears with a slightly different [color palette](/wiki/List_of_color_palettes_by_index_number_in_Generation_I "List of color palettes by index number in Generation I") (the palette index number 0x10) than usual when it is introduced by [Professor Oak](/wiki/Professor_Oak "Professor Oak"). This is the same red/yellow palette used for [Mew](/wiki/Mew_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Mew (Pokémon)"), [Mewtwo](/wiki/Mewtwo_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Mewtwo (Pokémon)"), and [Jynx](/wiki/Jynx_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Jynx (Pokémon)") (except the [Virtual Console](/wiki/Virtual_Console "Virtual Console") Jynx), as well as the [battle](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_battle "Pokémon battle") sprites of all [Pokémon Trainers](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trainer "Pokémon Trainer").
  * Due to the color palette used at the start of Pokémon Yellow, Pikachu has red cheeks (in the [Game Boy Color](/wiki/Game_Boy_Color "Game Boy Color")) or pink cheeks (in the [Super Game Boy](/wiki/Super_Game_Boy "Super Game Boy")). However, in this game's normal gameplay, Pikachu has dark orange cheeks instead.
- In [Pokémon Yellow](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version") and from [Generation VI](/wiki/Generation_VI "Generation VI") to [Brilliant Diamond and Shining Pearl](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Brilliant_Diamond_and_Shining_Pearl "Pokémon Brilliant Diamond and Shining Pearl"), Pikachu uses its [animated series](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_animated_series "Pokémon animated series") [cry](/wiki/Cry "Cry") ([voiced](/wiki/Voice_acting_in_the_Pok%C3%A9mon_games "Voice acting in the Pokémon games") by [Ikue Otani](/wiki/Ikue_Otani "Ikue Otani")). Before Pokémon: Let's Go, Pikachu! and Let's Go, Eevee!, it was the only Pokémon to do so in the core series games.
  * In [Pokémon Yellow](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version"), only the [first partner](/wiki/First_partner_Pok%C3%A9mon "First partner Pokémon") Pikachu uses the animated series cry (technically, this applies to all Pikachu [originally received or caught](/wiki/Original_Trainer "Original Trainer") by the player, but catching additional Pikachu is impossible without cheating) with [outsider](/wiki/Outsider_Pok%C3%A9mon "Outsider Pokémon") or NPC Pikachu using their original cries. In other games however all specimens of the species use the animated series cry.
  * Its battle cry differs from status or [Pokédex](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9dex "Pokédex") screens.
  * The [Pikachu](/wiki/Pikachu_(Yellow) "Pikachu (Yellow)") received at the beginning of [Pokémon Yellow](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Yellow_Version "Pokémon Yellow Version") has unique [voice clips](/wiki/Voice_acting_in_the_Pok%C3%A9mon_games "Voice acting in the Pokémon games") in [Pokémon Stadium](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Stadium "Pokémon Stadium") and [Stadium 2](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Stadium_2 "Pokémon Stadium 2").
  * From [Pokémon Legends: Arceus](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Legends:_Arceus "Pokémon Legends: Arceus") onward, Pikachu's cry switched to a slightly modified version of its pre-Gen VI cry.
- Pikachu is the only core series first partner Pokémon that does not have a 7:1 gender ratio.
- [Pokémon Black, White](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Black_and_White_Versions "Pokémon Black and White Versions"), [Black 2, and White 2](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Black_and_White_Versions_2 "Pokémon Black and White Versions 2") are the only core series games in which Pikachu cannot be obtained without transporting or through an event.
- Pikachu is the only not fully evolved Pokémon whose [base stats](/wiki/Base_stats "Base stats") increased in Generation VI, as well as the only Pokémon to receive more than one stat increase: Defense and Special Defense.
- Pikachu is the 25th Pokémon in both the [National Pokédex](/wiki/National_Pok%C3%A9dex "National Pokédex") and [Alola Pokédex](/wiki/List_of_Pok%C3%A9mon_by_Alola_Pok%C3%A9dex_number_in_Pok%C3%A9mon_Sun_and_Moon "List of Pokémon by Alola Pokédex number in Pokémon Sun and Moon") in [Pokémon Sun and Moon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Sun_and_Moon "Pokémon Sun and Moon").
- In the Japanese [Pokémon Stadium](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Stadium_(Japanese) "Pokémon Stadium (Japanese)"), Pikachu is the only usable Pokémon that is not fully [evolved](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution").
- Pikachu is the only Pokémon that can be caught in all three pinball titles: [Pokémon Pinball](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Pinball "Pokémon Pinball"), [Pokémon Pinball mini](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Pinball_mini "Pokémon Pinball mini"), and [Pokémon Pinball: Ruby & Sapphire](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Pinball:_Ruby_%26_Sapphire "Pokémon Pinball: Ruby &amp; Sapphire").
- According to *[A Sneak Peek at Pokémon](/wiki/A_Sneak_Peek_at_Pok%C3%A9mon "A Sneak Peek at Pokémon")*, Pikachu can deliver a constant current of 1000 [amperes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ampere "wp:ampere").
- Pikachu is currently represented in the [Trading Card Game](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Trading_Card_Game "Pokémon Trading Card Game") by more cards than any other Pokémon, with 221 unique cards.


### Merchandise

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/d/db/Pikachu_Red_Battle_Chess.png)](/wiki/File:Pikachu_Red_Battle_Chess.png)Pikachu in Pokémon Battle Chess

- Pikachu appears in [Pokémon Battle Chess](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Battle_Chess#Pok%C3%A9mon_Battle_Chess "Pokémon Battle Chess"). It doesn't behave like any traditional piece in chess but behaves like a gold general in shogi.
- Pikachu was the focus of a [Pokémon Center](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Center_(store) "Pokémon Center (store)") [collection](/wiki/Sleeping_Pikachu_Collection "Sleeping Pikachu Collection") in June 2014.


### Real life

- The [Macy's Thanksgiving Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macy%27s_Thanksgiving_Day_Parade "wp:Macy's Thanksgiving Day Parade") has included three Pikachu balloons. "Flying Pikachu" was the first, flown from 2001 to 2005, and was used in a morning rush hour test of wind conditions in [Times Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Times_Square "wp:Times Square") after an accident involving an [M&M's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%26M%27s "wp:M&amp;M's") balloon in the 2005 parade.[[6]](#cite_note-6) It was last used in the August 2006 [Party of the Decade](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_10th_Anniversary "Pokémon 10th Anniversary") in New York City's [Bryant Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryant_Park "wp:Bryant Park"). The second balloon debuted in the 2006 parade, features glowing cheeks, and chases a [Poké Ball](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9_Ball_(item) "Poké Ball (item)") balloon. The third, introduced in the 2014 parade, wears a green scarf and holds a small, Pikachu-shaped snowman.


### Cultural impact

[![](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/media/upload/thumb/6/6b/Niue_Dollar_Pikachu.jpg/200px-Niue_Dollar_Pikachu.jpg)](/wiki/File:Niue_Dollar_Pikachu.jpg)$1 coin from [Niue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niue "wp:Niue") featuring Pikachu

- The nation of [Niue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niue "wp:Niue") released a one-dollar coin with its coat of arms on one side and Pikachu on the other to promote the [Pokémon](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon "Pokémon") franchise.
- Pikachu has served as [the inspiration for the naming](/wiki/List_of_references_to_Pok%C3%A9mon_in_science "List of references to Pokémon in science") of several scientific discoveries:
  * In 2008, Japanese researchers discovered a new protein that helps carry electrical impulses from the eyes to the brain and named it [pikachurin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/pikachurin "wp:pikachurin"). According to them, it was so named for sharing Pikachu's "lightning-fast moves and shocking electric effects".[[7]](#cite_note-7)
  * The [springtail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/springtail "wp:springtail") species *Dicranocentrus pikachu* was named after Pikachu.[[8]](#cite_note-8)
  * The spider species *Epicratinus pikachu* was named after Pikachu due to the female spider's [epigynum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epigyne "wp:Epigyne") resembling its face.[[9]](#cite_note-9)
  * The spider species *Alistra pikachu* was named after Pikachu due to its yellow coloration.[[10]](#cite_note-yejie_lin-10)
  * The beetle species *Hiperantha pikachu* was named after Pikachu because of its yellow elytra with a black apical band that resembles Pikachu's ears.[[11]](#cite_note-11)
- On December 11, 2023, [Google](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google "wp:Google") revealed that Pikachu was the most searched Pokémon in the platform's 25 years of history.[[12]](#cite_note-12)[[13]](#cite_note-13)


### Concept and development

Pikachu was designed by [Atsuko Nishida](/wiki/Atsuko_Nishida "Atsuko Nishida"), a core designer at [Game Freak](/wiki/Game_Freak "Game Freak").[[14]](#cite_note-14)[[15]](#cite_note-Pokemon.com-15) Pikachu had a short, stout design during the initial release of [Pokémon Red and Green](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_Red_and_Green "Pokémon Red and Green"), but its design was altered for *[Pokémon the Series](/wiki/Pok%C3%A9mon_the_Series "Pokémon the Series")* and subsequent entries of the games, becoming slightly taller and skinnier. According to [Ken Sugimori](/wiki/Ken_Sugimori "Ken Sugimori"), Pikachu's design was altered to make animating its actions easier.[[15]](#cite_note-Pokemon.com-15) Pikachu is depicted with a white belly similar to its evolved form, [Raichu](/wiki/Raichu_(Pok%C3%A9mon) "Raichu (Pokémon)"), in the [Bandai Pokémon Carddass Cards](/wiki/Bandai_Pok%C3%A9mon_Carddass_Cards "Bandai Pokémon Carddass Cards"), [as seen here](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/wiki/File:Bandai_Pikachu_card.jpg "a:File:Bandai Pikachu card.jpg").

Pikachu also originally had a second-stage [Evolution](/wiki/Evolution "Evolution") named [Gorochu](/wiki/List_of_unused_Pok%C3%A9mon_and_character_designs#Gorochu "List of unused Pokémon and character designs") (Japanese: **ゴロチュウ** *Gorochū*), which would have had two horns and fangs.[[16]](#cite_note-16)[[17]](#cite_note-SiliconEra-17) Sugimori stated that while there was nothing wrong with its appearance, it was nonetheless omitted from Pokémon Red and Green, as the staff found that the game balance was better without it, and the limited game space meant that some Pokémon originally designed to evolve twice were changed to evolve only once.[[15]](#cite_note-Pokemon.com-15)

[Gigantamax](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax") Pikachu was designed by [James Turner](/wiki/James_Turner "James Turner").[[18]](#cite_note-18)

### Origin

Pikachu was originally a [daifuku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/daifuku "wp:daifuku")-like creature with ears sticking out. Its black ear tips are remnants of this original concept.[[15]](#cite_note-Pokemon.com-15)[[17]](#cite_note-SiliconEra-17)

Nishida named Pikachu "without giving it much thought", and while it wasn't originally intended to be based on a [mouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/mouse "wp:mouse"), [Game Freak](/wiki/Game_Freak "Game Freak") CEO [Satoshi Tajiri](/wiki/Satoshi_Tajiri "Satoshi Tajiri") made the decision to base it on such. During the process of making Pikachu "cuter", as per the instructions of game designer [Kōji Nishino](/wiki/K%C5%8Dji_Nishino "Kōji Nishino"), Nishida added cheek pouches for it to store electricity in. These were inspired by [squirrels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/squirrel "wp:squirrel"), which store food in their cheeks. Pikachu's yellow coloration and lightning bolt-shaped tail were given to express Pikachu's electrical attributes and make its type easier to recognise and understand. According to Nishida, Pikachu's stripes aren't based on anything in particular, and were added to fill the empty space on Pikachu's back, as it is always visible during battle.[[15]](#cite_note-Pokemon.com-15)[[17]](#cite_note-SiliconEra-17)

Pikachu's [Gigantamax](/wiki/Gigantamax "Gigantamax") form is a reference to its earlier, more rotund design from [Generation I](/wiki/Generation_I "Generation I") and [II](/wiki/Generation_II "Generation II").[[19]](#cite_note-Chunkachu-19)

#### Name origin

Pikachu is a combination of ピカピカ *pikapika* ([onomatopoeia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/onomatopoeia "wp:onomatopoeia") for sparkle) and チューチュー *chūchū* (the sound of a mouse squeaking).[[15]](#cite_note-Pokemon.com-15)[[20]](#cite_note-20)
